漢唐 3Q26 獲利優於預期：漢唐 3Q26 營收 275.06 億元，
QoQ+5.47%；稅後純益 45.69 億元，QoQ+21.43%；稅 後 EPS 23.97
元。3Q26 稅後純益優於原先預估的 39.27 億元，主因毛利率優
於預期。 
漢唐目前在手訂單金額約 1,939 億元：漢唐 01~05/2026 年接單
合約金額約 674 億元，截至 05/2026 年已簽約未認列之金額(在
手訂單)約 1,939 億元。在手訂單約 24~36 個月可以認列大部分。
目前在手訂單金額的施工地區，有 2/3 在台灣，1/3 在海外。 
24~36 個月認列大部分的在手訂單可能是保守估計，2H26 在手
訂單金額可能還會再增加。 
漢唐對營運樂觀：漢唐表示目前建廠需求遠大於供給，因目前
人力短缺，尤其是在台灣，暫時沒辦法去接其他大型客戶的訂
單。由於 AI 與相關需求，現在客戶建廠的需求會延續到 2029
年，漢唐對整體產業的展望表示樂觀。 
群益預估漢唐 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 85.54 元 /117.99 元：群

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
永續環境是漢唐經營理念之綠色承諾，因應全球環保節能的需求，我們透過
工程設計、系統整合提升工程效率，減少工程過程中能耗。漢唐集成響應政
府綠色採購辦法，我們從原物料取得、工程施作、運輸配送、完工至客戶使
用階段，優先要求廠商提供低污染、可回收、再利用之環保產品，以達到廢
棄物減量之目標。 
漢唐集成根著台灣，從初創之施工安裝小公司成長為專業工程集成公司，有
賴於各地政通人和，因此，漢唐集成本著感恩的心回饋在地，不斷捐助弱勢
及支持培育英才，期望肩擔起企業之「社會責任」，讓台灣變得更美好。漢
唐股東會由全體股東組成，對公司重大事項進行決策，定期聽取董事會報
告，為公司最高決策機關；董事會為最高治理機關，董事會成員皆恪盡善良
管理人之注意義務，擘劃公司的經營政策與檢視財務績效，並確保公司營運
遵守各種法令；為完善公司治理運作、強化公司競爭力，董事會設置審計委
員會與薪資報酬委員會來健全董事會運作；董事會之下亦設有獨立之稽核
室，定期執行稽核業務並向審計委員會與董事會呈報稽核結果。 
公司簡介： 
漢唐集成成立於 1982 年，為一以「全面性整合服務」方式，專業提供高科
技產業建廠之系統集成服務公司；主要業務範圍包括高科技廠房各系統，包
括電腦、控制、無塵室、機電、電信系統之施作與整合服務。 
漢唐目前在手訂單金額約 1,939 億元： 
漢唐 01~05/2026 年接單合約金額約 674 億元，截至 05/2026 年已簽約未認
列之金額(在手訂單)約 1,939 億元。在手訂單約 24~36 個月可以認列大部
分。目前在手訂單金額的施工地區，有 2/3 在台灣，1/3 在海外。 
24~36 個月認列大部分的在手訂單可能是保守估計，2H26 在手訂單金額可
能還會再增加。 
漢唐未來還有先進封裝廠的訂單： 
大客戶於美國建廠的第三期工程的訂單目前漢唐已經有接到一部份，還沒
有認列到手訂單金額中，客戶的先進封裝廠的訂單還沒發包。第二期工程進
度希望在 2026 年認列約 85%，第三期工程的認列狀況要看客戶，未來還有
先進封裝廠的訂單。 
美國營收已超過台灣： 
漢唐沒有透露前兩大客戶的營收占比。2025 年營收占比_地區別：台灣 65%、
美國 25%、其他 9%。 1Q26 台灣 40%、美國 56%、其他 4%。美系記憶體廠
在台灣蓋無塵室，漢唐還是主要的供應商。 
漢唐對營運樂觀： 
漢唐表示目前建廠需求遠大於供給，因目前人力短缺，尤其是在台灣，暫時
沒辦法去接其他大型客戶的訂單。由於 AI 與相關需求，現在客戶建廠的需
求會延續到 2029 年，漢唐對整體產業的展望表示樂觀。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 116: 261112_daiwa_accton

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_daiwa_accton.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Accton’s 3Q26 results, released on 12 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat/miss expectations. Accton’s 3Q26 
net profit came in at TWD13.1bn (EPS: TWD23.3), in line with our estimate 
(TWD13.0bn) but 6% lower than the Bloomberg consensus (TWD13.9bn).  
Revenue was TWD120.7bn (+26% QoQ, 66% YoY), higher than our/BBG 
estimates by 13%/2%, mainly due to increasing AI card contribution for 
Trainium 3. Nonetheless, gross margin fell by 2.1pp QoQ to 17.6% (vs. our 
19.1% estimate), reflecting rising material costs (especially from memory). 
Consequently, OPM declined by 1.6pp to 13.4% (vs. our 15.1% estimate), 
which was slightly offset by better operating leverage.

Business outlook. We believe gross margin is likely the key highlight of 
the 3Q26 results. Per management, gross margin was diluted due to rising 
memory costs. As such, Accton has shifted its business model from “Buy-&-
Sell” to consignment from September. Since then, we see gross margin 
gradually recovering back to the 20% level. Looking into 4Q26, we believe 
Accton could sustain the gross margin, but the networking chipset shortage 
might weigh on revenue momentum in the short term. The alternative is 
that one of the key switch clients could shift its orders slightly towards 
Spectrum first to ease the near-term shortage issue, in our view. As for the 
AI card business, we believe the total shipment has been largely stable 
since 3Q26. Even when the end-client gradually shifts its product mix from 
air-cooled to the liquid-cooled version, we believe the rising content from 
the liquid-cooled version should be able to offset the slightly lower ASP per 
chipset given there are more chipsets (4x vs. 2x from air-cooled) on an 
ASIC AI motherboard. Looking into 2027, we maintain our original view that 
total AMZN ASIC units will grow c.30% YoY to c.3m as the first growth 
drivers. On top of this, the liquid-cooled version has adopted the PCIE 
scale-up switch, which can expand TAM for Accton, in our view. Lastly, for 
the top-of-rack (ToR) switches, we expect 800G switches to remain the 
mainstream models in 2027 but with rising enterprise usage, which could 
likely be the next growth driver in 2027.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 6-12% to factor in 
higher ToR switch revenue. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating, but lower our 
12M TP to TWD3,000 (prior: TWD3,666), based on a target PER multiple of 
24x (prior: 35x, within its past-3-year range of 10-33x), applied to our 1-
year forward EPS forecast, to reflect the weaker market sentiment. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected ASIC server and switch demand.

How we differ: Our 2027E EPS is 5% higher than the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely due to our more positive view on operating leverage.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: gross margin dilution on memory costs

➢ Fundamentals largely on track with our prior estimates  
➢ Recent pullbacks provide good entry points to build positions 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12M TP to TWD3,000

Company profile 
Founded in 1988, Accton is dedicated to the design and manufacturing of enterprise networking and 
communication products. With its long-term experience and expertise, Accton is one of the leading 
open-source networking ODM partners for major players worldwide, with particular focuses on data 
centres, telco carriers and campus networks. Major products include enterprise switches, metro access 
switches, network applications and wireless products.

### Page 5

5

Accton Technology (2345 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Accton’s board has 7 directors, with 3 independent directors. The independent directors account
for 43% of all directors. A total of 8 board meetings were held in 2025, and the average actual
attendance rate was 95%.
The backgrounds of independent directors are diversified and their competence includes
business management, accounting, finance, and work experience in the medical industry. We
deem the diversified background as a positive factor for the company’s board quality.
Besides, the Chairman and President are not the same person, which increases the board and
executive management’s quality. Based on its board structure and key executive hiring, we
believe that Accton has already professionalised the family business by allowing external
professionals to run the business for major shareholders.
Capital management 1
Accton declared DPS of TWD15.0 in 2025, representing a 31.8% payout ratio. The ratio was
lower than the 51.2% in 2024 and its historical range of 69-75% from 2018 to 2022. That said, it
still points towards a friendly shareholder dividend policy, in our view.
We attribute the lower payout ratio mainly to the sharp earnings growth and continued capacity
investments in Taiwan and Vietnam amid US-China tensions. The DPS CAGR in the past 10
years was c.23%, which showcased Accton’s capability in capital management. The continuous
improvement in networking capabilities (eg, 400G and 800G switches), alongside expansion into
AI accelerator modules, has helped it differentiate itself from other competitors and strengthen its
position in the white-box switch supply chain for major US cloud service providers.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, revenue from related parties accounted for 0.15% of total revenue, while purchases
from related parties accounted for 2.0% of cost of sales. We see limited risk from related-party
transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
Like most vendors, Accton asks its suppliers to follow: 1) the hazard substance requirements of
the RoHS, Reach and corporate social responsibilities (5 categories, including labour, health and
safety, environmental, ethics and management system) required by the RBA Code of Conduct,
and 2) QC08000 (Hazardous Substances Process Management), while the company conducts
annual reviews to evaluate its suppliers’ performance.
To effectively track, communicate and manage its supply chain to manufacture green products,
each supplier can use the eGreen system® platform, developed independently by Accton, to
learn about environmental protection control and management requirements for components and
materials.
S Data security 2
To ensure information security for customers and the company, Accton obtained ISO/IEC
27001:2013 certification in 2016 and completed the transition to ISO/IEC 27001:2022 in 2024,
with continued verification of its information security policies and controls in 2025. Apart from the
global information security standards, Accton also takes measures to improve its information
security, including:1) Implementing business continuity and disaster recovery mechanisms,
including annual recovery drills for critical systems and databases. In 2025, the company
achieved its target of recovering critical systems within 48 hours. 2) Conducting monthly
vulnerability scans across company-wide servers, network equipment and core systems to
identify potential security risks. Accton also conducts quarterly social engineering drills to
strengthen employee awareness of cybersecurity threats.
In 2025, Accton conducted four phishing simulation exercises involving over 13,000 participant
attendances, achieving an average phishing safe rate of 93%. The company reported no major
information security incidents or personal data breaches during the year.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

## Document 146: 261117_daiwa_andes

Source PDF: all report pdf/261117_daiwa_andes.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following on our results Memo for Andes, we widen our 
2026E net loss and maintain our Hold (3) rating. Management aims for a 
profitable 4Q26 to cover YTD losses, yet we are unsure how sustainable its 
bottom-line turnaround will be in the near term, given still uncertain timing 
of royalty conversion despite enriched IP and customer portfolios. That 
said, we remain structurally positive on Andes’s outlook, thanks to its 
leading position in the RISC-V architecture well prepared to capitalise on 
the AI cycle from cloud to edge to physical.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes in a mixed bag. We widen our 
2026E net loss for Andes to factor in its higher-than-expected opex YTD, 
but we keep our 2027E EPS largely unchanged to factor in its frugal opex 
control starting from 3Q26. Though bottom-line turnaround looks still 
bumpy in the near term on its suboptimal operating leverage due to still 
suboptimal scale, as a result of still uncertain timing of royalty revenue 
conversion, we merit management’s opex control to speed up bottom-line 
turnaround. This, together with its enriched RISC-V IP portfolio and 
broadened customer base for market-share gain, should help sustain its 
turnaround from 2H27 when topline expands to improve earnings quality 
and investor confidence.

Patience still for a sustainable turnaround. Despite this year’s business 
hiccups on delayed royalty ramp and opex volatility, we remain positive on 
Andes’s structural outlook, given its leading position in the RISC-V 
architecture offering enriched high-performance/low-power compute IP as 
substitutes to Arm and x86 CPU counterparts to capitalise on the secular AI 
cycle, with a broad coverage of applications from accelerated compute and 
embedded control to automotive. Management’s comments support our 
view, as it expects the RISC-V’s open-source ecosystem to drive innovation 
allowing designs of instruction sets tailored to customers’ specific needs, 
which should help enhance customer stickiness while attracting new 
customers for market-share gains. That said, patience may be still needed 
for Andes’s monetisation to bear fruit for a sustainable turnaround.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we raise our 12M 
TP to TWD240 (from TWD180), still based on DCF methodology (WACC: 
18%, terminal growth: 3%) which we believe is appropriate for a startup IP 
company such as Andes. Given the still bumpy road to any sustainable 
turnaround, we maintain our Hold (3) call with key upside/downside risks of 
any faster RISC-V ecosystem build/further delays of royalty ramp.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 29-48% above the consensus 
forecast likely due to our lower opex assumptions for Andes.

17 November 2026

Near-term turnaround still looks bumpy

➢ 2026E net loss likely to widen on opex volatility but … 
➢ … no harm to structural turnaround from 2H27 
➢ Maintaining our Hold (3) rating with a higher 12M TP of TWD240

Company profile 
Founded in 2005, Andes is an embedded microprocessor intellectual property (IP) provider with 
solutions in high-performance and low-power 32/64-bit processors and associated SoC platforms. Its 
solutions can be adopted for diverse applications such as IoT, 5G, AI, Bluetooth/WiFi devices, gaming 
and automotive. As a founding member of RISC-V International, Andes focuses on developing 
processor IPs based on the RISC-V instruction-set architecture while its mature V3 architecture is the 
main contributor of its royalty revenue.

### Page 5

5

Andes Technology (6533 TT): 17 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 17 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Andes’ Board of Directors consists of seven members, including three independent directors,
representing approximately 43% of the total board composition, exceeding the regulatory
minimum requirement of 20%. The roles of Chairman and President are held by separate
individuals, reflecting a clear division of leadership responsibilities.
Currently, the Board includes one female director among the seven seats. Andes acknowledges
that gender diversity at the board level remains an area for improvement and is actively
committed to strengthening diversity and inclusion as part of its ongoing governance
enhancement efforts.
Capital management 1
As a semiconductor IP provider without physical manufacturing operations, Andes does not
require significant capital expenditure for factories. Instead, its primary capital needs are related
to ongoing investment in research and development to support new IP development and capture
emerging market opportunities. From this perspective, we believe Andes is inclined to retain a
portion of its earnings to fund future IP innovation rather than fully distributing profits to
shareholders.
Andes did not distribute dividends prior to 2019. In 2019, it paid out 72% of its 2018 net profit as
dividends. Subsequently, the dividend payout ratio remained high at approximately 90% during
the period from 2020 to 2022. However, the payout ratio declined sharply to 21% in 2023 based
on 2022 net profit, reflecting a more conservative capital allocation approach.
Due to weakened profitability, Andes did not distribute dividends in 2024 or 2025, as earnings
were insufficient to support shareholder payouts. Management has prioritized financial flexibility
and the preservation of resources to navigate challenging market conditions and sustain long-
term R&D investment.
Related party &
transaction 1
Before July 2021, Andes conducted related party transactions with MediaTek (2454 TT) given
each company's chairman was the same person. MediaTek was Andes' largest customer with a
31% revenue contribution in 2013. Its revenue contribution has gradually declined in past years
to a single digit percentage in 2021. Since Andes' board of directors' election in July 2021, its
chairman and MediaTek's chairman are no longer the same person. Andes currently does not
have any meaningful related party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
As a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, Andes mainly procures softwares &
services from its suppliers. Andes implements supplier management policies to ensure that there
is no violation of corporate social responsibility policy in the procurement contract with its
suppliers.
E Water & wastewater management 1
Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on-
site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment. It has set up a goal to reduce water
consumption by 2% every year through measures such as installing faucet water savers and
promoting the practice of saving water.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1 Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on- site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment.
E GHG emissions 1
Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on-
site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment. However, the company still applies
measures to reduce power consumption for its operations, such as turning off lights one hour per
day during the lunch break. It has set up a goal to reduce its total electricity consumption by 1-
2% every year.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

研 究 報 告                
旺矽(6223.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
探針卡需求強勁挹注短期營運動能強勁、CPO 設備進度如期  
 投資評價與建議 
目前探針卡廠商評價普遍修正至20~35倍，尤其以短期營運相對穩健之廠商
(如精測、旺矽)仍相對有撐，而我們觀察公司探針卡需求強勁支撐短期營運動
能強勁，並重申CPO設備驗證進度如期、客戶數持續增加，故仍看好評價仍有
望回到合理區間之中上緣。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率
維持近3Q26之高檔水準，觀察探針卡BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，且擴產
計畫不變。基於公司10月營收達22.3億元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，
預估營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年：
(1)VPC和MEMS明年底有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，擴充幅度仍具有上
修空間，當前VPC主要客戶未有升級至MEMS規劃，至於MEMS主要貢獻
則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU；(2)insertion 2客戶將於12月通知驗證結
果，1H27有望獲得量產機訂單，至於insertion 3已如期出貨工程機給3間
客戶，並預期1H27將陸續獲得量產機訂單，惟公司預估CPO設備營收顯
著貢獻時間點將落在2028年，故我們同步調整營收、獲利。 
 投資風險 
CPO進展不如預期、探針卡擴產針數下修。

### Page 2

| 營運概況 
3Q26獲利優於預期：3Q26營收季增12.8%至59億元，連續七季創下歷史新高，毛利率61.7%，稅後EPS 19.18元，
其中營收、獲利均優於預期，推估主因為探針卡產品組合改善，加上於2Q26進行之探針卡報價調整完整貢獻3Q
營收(包含CPC漲價20%、取消VPC和MEMS 10%價格優惠)，另公司營業費用管控得宜也同步挹注獲利。 
估4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率有望維持近3Q26之高檔水準，我們
推估動能包含：(1)維持VPC、MEMS月產能今年底分別擴至200萬針、350萬針之進展；(2)訂單出貨則觀察探針卡
BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，其中CPC今年前10個月維持滿載、目前BB Ratio接近1，至於MEMS、VPC則超過1，
考量公司探針卡交期已明顯延⻑至4~6個月，隱含本季探針卡營收仍有望持續提升。基於公司10月營收達22.3億
元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，預估本季營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年，我們觀察：(1)探針卡中，明年CPC月產能維
持50萬針、VPC和MEMS明年底則有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，產能擴充幅度具有上修空間，且強調2028年維持
積極擴產策略，訂單貢獻中，VPC主要客戶近年未有升級至MEMS探針卡之規劃，預期該客戶AI ASIC訂單將支撐
明年VPC需求，至於MEMS明年主要貢獻則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU，且公司持續爭取潛在客戶AI ASIC訂單，
另規劃於2H27量產之PCB廠有望挹注探針卡成本優化；(2)CPO設備中，insertion 2如我們先前所提，於3Q26底
～4Q26初送樣至客戶端進行驗證，公司預估客戶將於12月通知驗證結果，根據目前客戶回饋正向，預期1H27有
望獲得量產機訂單，此外近期有另⼀客戶與公司接洽中(公司預估1H27獲得驗證結果)，至於insertion 3則已如期
出貨工程機給3間客戶，也將陸續交機給另3間客戶，預計1H27將陸續獲得insertion 3量產機訂單，惟考量接單至
出貨、裝機驗收之時間較⻑，公司認為CPO相關設備顯著營收貢獻時間點將落在2028年，我們則同步下修明年相
關設備之營收認列數量。綜上所述，公司預估明年營收有望年增至少50%、毛利率維持在60%以上；我們則預估
明年營收有望年增58.6%至344.6億元，較先前下修主要反映部分CPO設備遞延至2028年認列營收，毛利率63.2%，
稅後EPS 117.77元。

## Document 204: [000270] 기아(000270) 3Q26 Review_ EV 판매 증가의 역풍기아(000270) 3Q26 Review_ EV 판매 증가의 역풍3Q26 Review_ EV 판매 증가의 역풍 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 3Q26 Review_ EV 판매 증가의 역풍기아(000270) 3Q26 Review_ EV 판매 증가의 역풍3Q26 Review_ EV 판매 증가의 역풍 - 기업.pdf

이재일 CFA 
6183 / lee.jae-il@eugenefn.com

3Q26 Review: EV 판매 증가의 역풍 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 160,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 95,600 원(10/30)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1%yoy), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%yoy, OPM 5.8%), 지배이익 1.29조원(-9.8%yoy)을 기록, 도매 판
매 증가로 인한 매출 증가에도 불구하고 인센티브/원자재 비용 증가로 인해 
컨센서스를 하회하는 실적 쇼크를 기록 
 영업이익 증감 요인: 인센티브&가격 -7,270억원, 믹스 -4,650억원, 재료비 
-3,680억원으로 총 1.56조원 비용 발생. 증가 요인은 판매량 +4,420억원, 
환율 +5,290억원, 기타비용 절감 +4,130억원으로 1.38조원 상쇄. 인센티
브로 인한 비용 증가 예상외로 컸는데 EV 판매량 증가 영향. 기말환율 하락
으로 판매보증충당금은 전년비 9,254억원 감소 
 전동화 믹스 증가: 글로벌 xEV 소매 판매 30만대(+47%yoy), 판매비중 
36.2%(+9.8%p yoy). HEV 17.2만대(+46%yoy), 텔루라이드, 스포티지 
HEV 공급 확대로 3분기 미국 HEV 판매 +118%yoy 기록. EV 12.3만대
(+75%yoy), 서유럽 EV 판매는 +163%yoy 기록 
 4분기 전망: 중동 분쟁 지속으로 원가 부담의 급격한 개선은 어려울 전망. 
연초 CID에서 제시한 연간 판매 목표는 달성 가능할 것으로 보이지만 기존 
수익성 목표를 하회할 전망. 중장기적으로 상품 경쟁력 강화, 지역별 파워트
레인 대응 전략 유지, 내부 원가 및 체질 개선으로 외부 비용 부담 상쇄를 
추진할 계획. 중동 분쟁 완화에 따른 비용 부담 해소와 HEV 비중 확대를 통
한 수익성 개선은 2027년 시점이 될 것으로 예상 
 단기 방향 전환 어려울 듯: 판매 호조에도 불구하고 저가형 EV 판매 비중 
증가함에 따라 수익성이 악화되는 모습이 3분기에도 재확인. 글로벌 수요 
변화를 감안했을 때, EV 시장 선점이 매우 중요한 것은 사실이지만 단기 수
익성 부담 커지고 있는 점 우려됨. 중국산 EV의 글로벌 확산, 중동 전쟁 장
기화에 따른 원가 부담 증대가 연말까지는 영향을 미칠 것. 동사에 대한 투
자의견 매수와 목표주가 16만원 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 253: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업.pdf

업

 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 
이지니

jini.lee@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중

## Document 261: AI 投研时代，行情变快了吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI 投研时代，行情变快了吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

天⻛证券通过对⽐本轮科技股调整与历史⽜市⻅顶，结合申万⼆级⾏业数据得出三点结
论：⼀、有业绩⽀撑的周期股逻辑⻅底更快、跌幅更深；⼆、 AI 投研时代⾏情⻅底时间整
体拉⻓；三、⽆业绩⽀撑的纯叙事上涨，下跌共识形成速度加快。
摘要：
核⼼结论
AI 投研时代，⾏情发展的速度是否会受到影响？把⾃六⽉底以来的调整与历史上不同⽜市⻅顶
下跌放在同⼀组价格路径指标下⽐较，综合下跌速度和共识形成的速度，结合本轮科技⾏情既
具备类似  2007-2008 的周期股逻辑也具备类似  2015 年移动互联⽹的产业叙事逻辑，我们认为
本轮调整可以理解为与⾏情结构基本相符。
扩展到更多⾏业以及更普遍的⻅顶回撤，得出以下三点结论：
正⽂摘要
AI 投研时代，⾏情发展的速度是否会受到影响？本⽂通过两步回答这个问题，先把⾃六⽉底以
来的调整与历史上不同⽜市⻅顶下跌放在同⼀组价格路径指标下⽐较，回答 “ 这次是否不⼀样 ” ；
再扩展到历史上多轮周期⾏业与  叙事⽀撑估值的⾏业⻅顶调整，⽐较两种调整之间、 AI 投研时

代与前  AI 投研时代之间的区别。
下跌速度⽽⾔，本轮相较可⽐样本没有额外加速特征。调整前置程度⽽⾔，  A 股可⽐样本中不
少  D60/D90 接近  100% ，或意味着历史上  60 个交易⽇差不多初步⻅⽀撑，这次数字更⼩但
T+N ⽇累积跌幅却没有显著偏离，我们认为可以初步表明本轮⽤ “ 历史相近的速率调整了更久 ” 。
综合下跌速度和共识形成的速度，结合本轮科技⾏情既具备类似  2007-2008 的周期股逻辑也具
备类似  2015 年移动互联⽹的产业叙事逻辑，本轮调整可以理解为与⾏情结构基本相符。
本轮科技⾏情兼具周期股逻辑与产业叙事逻辑，为了讨论这两种逻辑⽀撑的上涨之间、有⽆  AI
投研影响之间，是否存在下跌速率的变化。
本⽂从申万⼆级⾏业符合⻅顶下跌标准的⾏情段中分别： 1 ）取周期股⾏业且有业绩⽀撑的⾏情
段作为周期股逻辑样本， 2 ）取⾮周期⾏业且不存在业绩⽀撑的⾏情段作  为纯叙事拔估值逻辑的
观察样本。得出以下三点结论：
有业绩周期股逻辑相⽐纯叙事拔估值逻辑，⻅底更快、跌幅更深；

AI 投研时代，⾏情⻅顶下跌的⻅底时间或更⻓；
AI 投研时代，没有业绩⽀撑的纯叙事上涨，下跌共识或形成更快。
结论⼀：有业绩周期股逻辑相⽐纯叙事拔估值逻辑，⻅底更快、跌幅更深。
结论⼆： AI 投研时代，⾏情⻅顶下跌的⻅底时间或更⻓。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

AI 投研时代，⾏情变快了吗？
AI 投研时代，⾏情发展的速度是否会受到影响？本⽂通过两步回答这个问题，先把⾃六⽉底以
来的调整与历史上不同⽜市⻅顶下跌放在同⼀组价格路径指标下⽐较，回答 “ 这次是  否不⼀样 ” ；
再扩展到历史上多轮周期⾏业与叙事⽀撑估值的⾏业⻅顶调整，⽐较两种调整之间、 AI 投研时
代与前  AI 投研时代之间的区别。
历史⽜市⻅顶⽐较：本轮调整与⾏情结构基本相符
如何衡量⾏情发展的速度，即所谓 “ 跌的快 ” ？这个问题可以分成，是固定窗⼝跌幅更⼤，  还是最
猛烈的⼏天更陡，还是跌幅前置程度更深。前两者衡量下跌的速度，后者衡量共识形成的速
度。
下跌速度⽽⾔，本轮相较可⽐样本没有额外加速特征。最差  N ⽇跌幅结果显⽰，本轮⾏业和个
股确有剧烈调整段，但历史上也出现过更深的连续窗⼝如  2007-2008 年的煤炭、有⾊，以及
2015 年移动互联⽹⾏业的最差  5/10/20 ⽇回撤整体不弱于本轮； 2000 年前后费城半导体指数
的⻓窗⼝回撤也很深。
⾃⻅顶后  N 个交易⽇的累积跌幅、⾸次触及  10%/20%/30% 所需交易⽇也同样呈现处于历史较

快⽔平，但仍在常规范畴的特征。
调整前置程度⽽⾔，⽐较前  M ⽇的最⼤回撤 / 前  N ⽇的最⼤回撤 (M<N ），衡量是不是共识形成
更快，如  D5/D20 为⻅顶后  5 交易⽇最⼤收盘回撤 / ⻅顶后  20 交易⽇最⼤收盘回撤。 A 股可⽐样
本中不少  D60/D90 接近  100% ，即  90 ⽇内的最⼤回撤⼤多已在前  60 ⽇出现。这或意味着历史
上  60 个交易⽇差不多初步⻅⽀撑，这次数字更⼩但  T+N ⽇累积跌幅却没有显著偏离，我们认
为可以初步表明本轮⽤ “ 历史相近的速率调整了更久 ” 。
综合下跌速度和共识形成的速度，结合本轮科技⾏情既具备类似  2007-2008 的周期股逻辑也具
备类似  2015 年移动互联⽹的产业叙事逻辑，我们认为本轮调整可以理解为与⾏情结构基本相
符。
结论三： AI 投研时代，没有业绩⽀撑的纯叙事上涨，下跌共识或形成更快。

### Page 3

两类⾏业⻅顶回撤⽐较： AI 投研时代⾏情速率有何变化
第⼀部分我们观察到，本轮调整与⾏情结构基本相符，但或存在调整时间更久的特征。
考虑到上⽂所选样本仅为历史上较⼤级别⽜市的⻅顶，要想回答是否速率变快以及确认 “ 调整时
间更久 ” 是否准确，我们认为需要扩展到更多⾏业以及更普遍的⻅顶回撤。
本轮科技⾏情兼具周期股逻辑与产业叙事逻辑，为了讨论这两种逻辑⽀撑的上涨之间、有⽆  AI
投研影响之间，是否存在下跌速率的变化。
本⽂从申万⼆级⾏业符合⻅顶下跌标准的⾏情段中分别： 1 ）取周期股⾏业且有业绩⽀撑的⾏情
段作为周期股逻辑样本， 2 ）取⾮周期⾏业且不存在业绩⽀撑的⾏情段作为纯叙事拔估值逻辑的
观察样本。
定义 “ ⻅顶下跌 ” 的标准为： 1 ）前置上涨：阶段⾼点⾄少⽐此前  252 个交易⽇内的最低收盘价⾼
30% ； 2 ）⻅顶下跌触发：从该阶段⾼点回撤达到  20% ； 3 ）事件结束：从低点反弹  10% 或者当
前距离低点已经过去半年。
定义 “ 有业绩⽀撑 ” 的标准为：⻅顶所在季度前后各两个季度的  5 个季度⾥，⾄少出现连续两个

季度同时满⾜⾏业营收 TTM 同⽐⼤于 0 、⽑利率 TTM 环⽐上升的公司占⽐⼤于 50% 。
⽤  2024/1/1 作为有⽆  AI 投研影响的分界线，并⽤  2025/1/1 做为敏感性分析。
结论⼀：有业绩周期股逻辑相⽐纯叙事拔估值逻辑，⻅底更快、跌幅更深
有业绩周期股逻辑与纯叙事拔估值逻辑横向⽐较，有业绩周期股逻辑的⾏情⻅底更快，跌幅更
深。
有业绩周期股逻辑在  2024 年以前的⻅底所⽤交易⽇中位数为  35 ⽇， 2024 年后  延伸⾄  40
⽇；纯叙事拔估值逻辑  2024 年以前的⻅底所⽤交易⽇中位数为  45 ⽇， 2024 年  后延伸⾄  81
⽇。
周期股⻅顶后第  5/20/30 交易⽇跌幅通常更深， 24 年前 / 后有业绩周期股  逻辑  D5/20/30 最⼤收
盘回撤中位数分别为 -10.7%/-21%/-28.3% 及 -8.8%/-15.6%/-20.9% ；⽽纯叙事拔估值逻辑各个维
度的中位数最⼤收盘回撤均更⼩。
以  2025/1/1 为分界线后，两组中位数同样⽀持有业绩周期股逻辑⻅顶⾄最低点所⽤交易⽇更

短， D5/20/30 最⼤回撤更深的结论。

### Page 4

结论⼆： AI 投研时代，⾏情⻅顶下跌的⻅底时间或更⻓
各个⼝径⽐较下，均显⽰进⼊  24/25 年后，⻅顶下跌的过程被⼀定程度拉⻓。两个截断⼝径的
⽅向相同，⽆论是周期⾏业 / ⾮周期⾏业全体，还是有业绩⽀撑的周期股逻辑与纯叙事拔估值逻
辑样本下，⻅底所需时间⼤多更⻓。
但最⼤跌幅中位数并没有随时间延⻓⽽同步加深， D30 回撤总体相近或更浅，因⽽更准确的描
述是下跌过程拉⻓，不是近期跌得更深或全程更快。
结论三： AI 投研时代，没有业绩⽀撑的纯叙事上涨，下跌共识或形成更快
D5/D20 跌幅⽐衡量的是⻅顶后  5 ⽇与  20 ⽇最深跌幅之⽐，数值越⼤则说明价格的下修在  前  5
个交易⽇更集中，可以作为对共识形成速度的代理变量。
纯叙事拔估值逻辑的  D5/D20 中位数在  24 年截断下从  45.9% 提升到  51.6% ，在  25 年截断下从
45.9% 提升到  60.7% ；没有业绩⽀撑的周期⾏业也有同样规律。对于有业绩周期股逻辑，则不
⽀持此种观察。
注意截断到  25 年后，前置程度提升更⾼，某种程度上说明在  AI 投研更完善的时间，纯叙事上

涨的下跌共识形成更快。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1800 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1720 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1680 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 318: DeepSeek前成员张博开源Linger项目，主打零token费

Source PDF: all report pdf/DeepSeek前成员张博开源Linger项目，主打零token费.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

11 ⽉ 19 ⽇，前 DeepSeek-VL 项⽬负责⼈张博在 X 宣布开源个⼈ Agent Linger ，开发主体是
张博创办的 Simplex AI 。零 token 费是 Simplex AI 主打的卖点， README 与官⽹均写明本
地推理不产⽣云端账单和 token 费⽤。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 19 ⽇，前 DeepSeek-VL 项⽬负责⼈张博在 X 宣布开源个⼈ Agent Linger ，开发主体是张博创
办的 SIMPLEX AI TECHNOLOGIES PTE. LTD. （以下简称 “Simplex AI” ），代码已上传 GitHub 。
按项⽬⽂档， Linger 读取本地邮件、⽇历和⽂件，操作⽤⼾已登录的浏览器；连接外部应⽤的读
取直接执⾏，发邮件等写⼊操作才弹出确认卡，⽤⼾可随时接管。
权限边界也写进了官⽅⽂档。 Agent 以⽤⼾授权的运⾏环境执⾏任务，可访问该电脑的⽂件、已
登录应⽤和命令⾏。 Linger ⽤确认卡和服务端校验限制 Agent 动作，没有采⽤沙箱隔离。

按 Simplex AI 公布的实验室测试数据，在 12GB 显存加 64GB 内存的配置下，阿⾥千问 Qwen3.8-
Flash-Next 的⽣成速度为 45.5 tokens/ 秒，该速度为模型开始⽣成后的稳态速度，不含⾸ token 等
待。零 token 费是 Simplex AI 主打的卖点， README 与官⽹均写明本地推理不产⽣云端账单和
token 费⽤。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

这组性能数字存在不同测试⼝径，实际使⽤效果仍待验证。
让 125B 模型装进 12GB 显卡的是 PolyStrata ，⼀个 MoE 推理引擎。⽂档将其原理描述为分层管理
专家权重，部分专家放在显存，其余放在系统内存或 SSD ，⽤到时再调度。 Qwen3.8-Flash-
Next 的 2-bit 量化⽂件约 39GB ，这些权重靠分层调度运⾏，不会⼀次性载⼊显存。
官⽹显⽰， PolyStrata 已适配 12 个开源模型，包括 Qwen 、 DeepSeek 、 Gemma 、 Qwen3.8-
Flash-Next 、 Qwen3.8-27B 和 Spark-X2.6-4B ，另有更多模型在列。发布会⽤于演⽰的千问模型
只是其中之⼀， DeepSeek 同样在⽀持列表内。对本地推理⽤⼾来说，模型选择不绑定单⼀⼚
商，可按硬件条件切换，也不必为某⼀家模型的云端调⽤单独订阅。
成本账的重要前提是硬件。 45.5 tokens/ 秒对应的是 12GB 显卡加 64GB 内存。
官⽅另⼀组硬件规划测试显⽰， 12GB 显卡配 32GB 内存的模拟配置下，同⼀模型速度为 21.3

tokens/ 秒，⾸字延迟约 3 分钟。这组数据来⾃ 96GB 显卡限制显存预算的模拟，并⾮真实 12GB
显卡实测，该档默认推荐模型其实是 Qwen3.6-35B-A3B 。两项数据分属不同测试，不能直接对
照。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

8GB 显卡档标注的 38.1 tokens/ 秒，脚注同样注明是在 96GB 显卡上限制到 8GB 预算测得，不是
真实 8GB 显卡。
官⽅⽂档写明，⼯作数据存放在⽤⼾⾃⼰运⾏的服务器与电脑上，但有⼏类数据会离机。使⽤
远程模型时，模型调⽤把提⽰词和上下⽂发给模型服务商。连接 Gmail 、 Slack 等应⽤、语⾳识
别和实时语⾳分别调⽤ Composio 、 Soniox 和 xAI 。后台记忆和 Wiki ⽣成⽤的是 Claude Code 或
Codex 等运⾏时。
Linger 的省账限于本地模型推理⽆需⽀付 token 费。硬件、电⼒和上述外部服务的成本另计。
商业模式上， Linger 把本地部署开放给个⼈使⽤，同时保留云端托管权。当前不少个⼈ AI 助⼿及

Agent 产品采⽤云端模型或托管服务模式，⽤⼾通过订阅、⽤量付费或⾃备 API 额度使⽤；云端
模型通常要把任务所需的上下⽂发给模型服务商。
Linger 开源版本可免费在本地使⽤，另设 hosted 版本，⽬前只开放等候名单，未公布定价。截⾄
11 ⽉ 20 ⽇，该项⽬ GitHub 仓库星标约 1.8k 。
项⽬许可证以 Apache 2.0 为基础增加了商业限制。未经授权，其他企业不得把 Linger 源码作为托
管服务提供给外部⽤⼾，也不得删改前端的 Logo 与版权信息。这⼀条款把本地使⽤放开、把第
三⽅托管收紧，商业化空间留给 Simplex AI ⾃⼰的 hosted 服务。
张博在开源帖中称，如今 AI 在数据中⼼训练，再以 token 的形式租回给⽤⼾，智能应该由⽤⼾完
全拥有。与 DeepSeek 此前展⽰的 DS-Bot 相⽐，他回应称， DS-Bot 的核⼼是 Bot 与 Bot 、 Bot 与群
组的协作， Linger 的核⼼是⼈与⾃⼰的 Agent 、 Agent 与本地环境。

张博 2019 年在⾹港科技⼤学取得博⼠学位，随后进⼊微软亚洲研究院研究计算机视觉， 2023 年
加⼊ DeepSeek ，任 DeepSeek-VL 项⽬负责⼈，离开后在浙江⼤学任教并创办 Simplex AI 。
这款产品仍处在开源早期，⽀撑性能说法的⼏组数字为企业⾃测⼝径，暂⽆第三⽅复现。后续
值得关注的是， Linger 到 2026 年底的 GitHub 星标量级，以及有⽆第三⽅复现 12GB 跑 125B 达
45.5 tokens/ 秒的效果。

## Document 321: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

53.2% for our CIO Foundation Balanced portfolio 
Foundation tactical asset allocation

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

## Document 336: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-20).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
OCTOBER CPI PREVIEW
Inflationary pressure fades as we move through year-end
17 November 2026
Key points
• October CPI to rise 0.41% with the annual 
pace lifting to 4.8%yr. The seasonally 
adjusted CPI lifts 0.7%mth, a small step 
up from the 0.6% increase in September.  
• October Trimmed Mean (TM) is set 
to gain 0.42%mth lifting the annual 
pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr. 
• There is a small downside revision 
from our October 19th estimate for 
the December quarter CPI to 1.2%qtr 
while the TM remains 0.9%qtr.  
• The December quarter will represent the 
peak in Australian inflationary pressure. 
Our March quarter CPI remains 0.4%qtr 
and the TM is unchanged at 0.7%qtr. 
• We now see TM inflation dipping below 
3% by Q3 2027 and be well within the 
RBA’s target band by end 2027 at 2.7%yr.
• Demand remains the larger driver of 
inflation, but the recent pick-up in 
trimmed mean inflation appears to 
have been largely supply-driven.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Inflation accelerated in September...
The CPI rose 0.8% in the month of September, stronger than 
Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the market’s expectation of 
0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr. The 
seasonally adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting 
some positive seasonality in the month. 
Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: auto 
fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday travel & accommodation 
(8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation 
(7.2%mth); and property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There 
were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–3.1%mth); 
household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The more important underlying measure, the Trimmed Mean 
(TM), gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and 
market expectations with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr 
(revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised 
pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this 
series.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second 
consecutive month. The share of the basket running above 
3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, while the share below 2%yr 
rose from 27% to 32%. Meanwhile, our replication of the San 
Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum index moved into 
positive territory after trending higher since April. Around 
14% of the CPI basket, on an expenditure-weighted basis, 
experienced positive inflation shocks in September, driven 
by holiday travel, fuel, and clothing & footwear. This was 
partly offset by negative shocks across 7% of the basket, 
concentrated in food & non-alcoholic beverages. For full 
analysis of the September CPI, see “ Inflation accelerating in 
the run up to Christmas”.
... but is set to peak in October.
Looking ahead to October, early indicators were mixed. The 
NAB business survey suggested firms continued to absorb 
a meaningful share of higher input costs, with purchase cost 
growth edging up to 2.4%qtr (+0.1ppt from September), while 
retail price growth slowed sharply to 0.2%qtr (–0.8ppts), 
partly reflecting some moderation in labour cost growth 
(see here). Meanwhile, consumers’ year-ahead inflation 
expectations rose to 5.3% in October, with this rate holding 
steady in November. Fuel prices provided a more pronounced 
source of inflationary pressure, with retail petrol and diesel 
prices jumping 8.7% and 5.3%, respectively, in October to 
average $2.40/l and $2.82/l as global oil prices approached a 
local peak.
October to mark the peak in inflation
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.4% (0.41% to 2dp), lifting
annual inflation to 4.8%, which we expect to mark the peak.
On a seasonally adjusted basis, we estimate a stronger 0.7%
increase (0.68% to 2dp). For the monthly trimmed mean, we

expect a 0.4% rise (0.42% to 2dp), nudging the annual pace up
to 3.7%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking a 1.0% increase
in October. While conceptually different from the quarterly
trimmed mean, this measure nonetheless provides a useful
guide to near-term underlying inflation momentum. For market
services excluding volatiles & holiday travel, we have pencilled
in a 0.3% rise, lifting annual inflation to 4.0% from 3.8%,
which had marked a ten-month low.

Transport is expected to make the largest contribution
(+0.35ppts), almost entirely reflecting auto fuel (+0.32ppts).
Smaller contributions are expected from clothing & footwear
(+0.06ppts), household goods & services (+0.05ppts), and
meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The main offset comes
from recreation & culture (–0.18ppts), driven entirely by
holiday travel (–0.18ppts), while fruit & vegetables, utilities
and several other components provide only marginal offsets.

Key details for the month of October
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to rise 0.2% in
October, lifting annual inflation to 3.3%. Strength in the month
is concentrated in meals out & takeaway, where we expect
prices to rise 0.6%mth and 4.3%yr. We have allowed for a
greater-than-usual degree of price resetting as businesses
adjust to the card surcharging ban.
Housing inflation is expected to rise 0.1% in October, with the
annual pace increasing to 6.2% reflecting base effects from

electricity. Across the major components:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.2%mth and 3.5%yr, with the
late-September indexation of the Commonwealth Rent
Assistance flowing through more broadly in October. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate is stronger at 0.4%mth.
• New dwelling inflation has been a key source of downside
surprise in recent months. We expect a 0.2%mth rise
in October, which would see the annual pace moderate
to 4.5%. The downturn in established home prices has

deepened into year-end (see here), which should weigh on
new dwelling inflation over the months ahead. Our current
estimates point to a trough of around 1.3%yr by late-2027.
• Maintenance & repair costs are expected rise a solid
0.9%mth, taking annual inflation to 3.5%, while gas & other
household fuels provide the main offset, falling 2.5%mth.
Transport is expected to be the main source of upward
pressure in October, rising 2.9%mth and lifting to 11.6%yr.

This largely reflects an 8.2%mth jump in auto fuel prices
following the rise in global oil prices through the month. Motor
vehicle prices and spare parts are also expected to increase, by
0.4%mth and 1.6%mth respectively.

Recreation & culture is expected to provide the key offset, 
falling 1.5%mth, driven by a 3.0%mth decline in holiday travel. 
We also expect some pull-back across components exposed 
to the AI trade, including audio, visual & computing equipment 
(–0.2%mth) and games, toys & hobbies (–0.3%mth).
Underlying inflation holds momentum
As noted above, our October TM estimate is also 0.4%mth, a step 
up from 0.3% in September taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 
3.6%yr and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 
4.0%yr, the fastest pace in the short history of the series. Rents 
and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
Upside risks to October baseline ...
We view the risks around the October CPI outcome as slightly 
tilted to the upside. Our estimates already allow for a greater 
degree of price resetting in areas such as meals out & takeaway 
and personal and household services, but there is a risk that this 
adjustment proves broader than we have assumed. Increases in 
fuel levy surcharges in early October are another source of upside 
risk, including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post, with 
the latter nearly doubling its surcharge between September and 
October. The key downside risk remains housing, particularly 
new dwelling prices, which have surprised to the downside in 
recent months and could continue to do so as the housing market 
downturn deepens. Trade notifications data also point to some 
further downside risk, with builders on average announcing 
smaller price changes than in prior months.
... but momentum to ease in November ...
We have a preliminary estimate for the November CPI of 
–0.1%mth which will see the annual pace ease back to 4.6%yr. 
Auto fuel moved lower through November to be down –2.1% in 
the month with seasonal falls in garments, health, furniture & 
household appliances and holiday travel offsetting some strength 
in food, housing, communication and financial services. 
Seasonally adjusted the CPI is estimated to lift 0.1% in November, 
highlighting the seasonal weakness in the month, while our TM 
estimate is for a 0.2% lift seeing the annual pace ease a touch to 
3.6%yr while the six month annualised pace drops from 4.2%yr 
to 3.9%yr. This moderation is expected to continue into December 
with a preliminary TM estimate of 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. 
The following page has a table with monthly profiles for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to July 2027. By July 
2027 we now expect an annual pace of 3.3%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI while our estimate for the TM is 3.0%yr. 
More significantly, the six month annualised pace of the TM is 
estimated to be down to 2.7%yr by June 2027, well within the 
RBA’s target band. We have also included tables breaking down 
the monthly components of the CPI through the December and 
March quarters. 
... with December quarter now the peak ...
Our December quarter CPI estimate has been revised down 
slightly to 1.2%qtr, a modest 0.05ppt revision, with the annual 
pace lifting from 3.9%yr to 4.6%yr. The seasonally adjusted

CPI gains 1.4% in the December quarter highlighting normally
December is a seasonally soft quarter. Our TM estimate for
Q3 has a small downward revision of 0.05ppt to 0.85% at two
decimal place but this rounds up to our published estimate of
0.9%qtr. For now that suggests downside risk for our TM

estimate as getting the quarter to round to 0.8% is much
easier than getting it to round up to 1.0% . We also note
auto fuel prices falling faster than expected which may not
only result in downside risks to out CPI estimate but also our
TM estimate due to less pass through pressure.
... with inflation moderating through 2027 ...
Westpac’s preliminary March quarter CPI estimate is just 0.4%.
Driving most of this outcome is the 7.7% decline in autofuel

(–0.29ppt) and a 3.8% fall in holiday travel (–0.23ppt) being
offset by rising food (1.0%, 0.18ppt), housing (0.7%, 0.15ppt),
health (2.6%, 0.18ppt) and education (3.5%, 0.17ppt). The
March quarter does not have a lot in the way of net seasonally
with the seasonally adjusted estimate up 0.5%. Our underlying
TM measure of inflation is estimated to rise 0.7% in the March
quarter taking the annual pace down to 3.5% while the six
month annualised pace dips from 3.8%yr to 3.2%yr.

... as a disinflationary impulse kicks in.
We have for some time expected auto fuel prices to peak in
October, 240 cpl for petrol and 282 cpl for diesel, then to
trend lower from there. Our November estimates are currently
sitting on 237 cpl and 279 cpl respectively trending down to
186 cpl for petrol and 196 cpl for diesel by December 2027.
This represents a 12.7% fall in auto fuel through the first half
of 2027 subtracting 0.42ppt off the CPI over the same period

of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the
auto fuels profile please see: “Persistent energy pressures
have further to run”. At this stage we have not adjusted our
Brent forecast in line with the current correction underway
(see the November Market Outlook for more details why) as
we expect this to be a volatile ride. Should the current correc-
tion continue, auto fuel will represent a larger drag on the CPI
and potentially the Trimmed Mean.

Critical to our expected moderation in core inflation is a step
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab-
lished home prices. From a 1.0%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we
see dwelling price increasing by 0.4% in Q4 2026, and then
0.5% in Q1 and 0.4%qtr in Q2 before dropping to 0.2%qtr in
Q3 and Q4. This will see dwelling price inflation in the CPI
drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 2.3%yr by June 2027 then

down to 1.3%yr by December 2027.
At least to the end of this year there is still a high degree of
uncertainty about how much businesses will be able to pass
through upstream cost pressures. The latest NAB business sur-
vey continues to highlight robust input price pressure but also

that firms are struggling to pass on those increases.
For businesses, it is almost a now or never opportunity to raise
prices. We expect that through 2027, as energy prices ease
and the domestic economy slows under the weight of higher
interest rates and falling established home prices, households
will be much more resistant to price increases especially if dis-
cretionary household spending softens by more than expected.
That said, these headwinds may be at least partially offset by

the stronger starting point for household incomes, which has
proven more resilient than previously anticipated, and a possi-
ble bounce back in energy prices due to renewed conflict in the
Middle East.
Supply or demand driven inflation?
Inflation has steadily drifted away from the RBA’s target over
recent quarters. While the demand side is the larger driver of
inflation currently, most of the recent pick-up in TM inflation
appears to be from the supply side, and the more detailed

breakdown may understate how much.
There are several ways to split inflation into supply and de-
mand. One is to estimate how much of the rise a standard de-
mand-driven model can explain and attribute the rest to supply
(see here). Run through to Q3 2026, this approach puts most of
the recent pick-up in TM inflation down to supply. Without it,
trimmed mean would have risen from around 3.0%yr to 3.2%yr
over the year to Q3 2026, rather than the 2.9%yr to 3.7%yr

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
rise we actually saw. The supply contribution is moderate,
though, and well below its Covid-era peak. This analysis also
reveals that most of the disinflation through late-2023 and into
early-2025 reflected supply pressures unwinding, while de-
mand-driven inflation remained stickier, persisting above 3%yr.

Another is the San Francisco Fed approach, which the RBA has
also replicated (see here). The attribution is that if prices rise
and quantities fall, it is supply; if both rise, it is demand. We run
it to Q2 2026, as Q3 volume data are not yet available. Like the
first approach, it finds that demand accounts for most of infla-
tion (headline in this case). However, unlike the first approach,
it shows supply contributions easing a little.
A closer look at the categories classified as demand tells a

more nuanced story. Utilities, health and insurance all feature,
and their prices are largely shaped by government policy or are
not the categories that usually come to mind when people talk
about demand-driven inflation, and they are not very sensitive
to interest rates. You can look at electricity as a clear example
where prices in recent history have been set by government
policies (the introduction of, and then removal of, cost of living
rebates as well as policies to decarbonise the economy) or ex-

ternal supply shock (rising gas prices pushing up the marginal
cost of generation).
Categories more commonly thought of discretionary do show
up on the demand side, with recreation & culture (excluding
travel) and meals out & takeaway being the key ones. The
drawback of this sort of decomposition is that it labels cate-
gories where prices and quantities move together as demand,
even when this may reflect the ripple effects of a supply shock
elsewhere, a point our Chief Economist Luci Ellis detailed in a

note in 2024 (see In a world gone bananas, don’t squash the
other fruit). Some of this may be occurring now. In particular,
the analysis is likely not capturing the shift in spending towards
these categories resulting from travel disruptions associated
with the Middle East conflict (travel services itself was classi-
fied as a supply-side contributor in Q2). As travel normalises,
some of the “demand” contribution from recreation & culture
and meals out & takeaway should therefore fade over coming

quarters.
We would also acknowledge the work of Tim Toohey and his
analysis “The RBA has an identity problem”.

## Document 389: Productivity_ reasons for optimism (2026-11-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Productivity_ reasons for optimism (2026-11-20).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 15
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#gdp #australia #employment #housing #rba #productivity
Productivity: reasons for optimism
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
08:37 November 17 2026 Share
The supply-side outlook is improving as capital deepening strengthens, reallocation drags
fade and AI diffusion gathers pace.
The supply-side outlook is looking brighter. A pickup in capital deepening and a fading ‘reallocation’
drag are already evident in the Annual National Accounts, with AI diffusion offering further upside.

This gives us greater conviction that productivity growth can return towards 1% or more over time,

### Page 2

Growth in 2024-25 was a little firmer than first thought
The 2025–26 figures show somewhat firmer economic activity and business investment than previously
estimated, particularly in 2024–25. The economy grew by 2.3% in year-average terms, broadly in line
with the initial quarterly National Accounts estimate of 2.4%. However, growth in 2024–25 was revised
up by 0.3ppts to 1.6%, largely reflecting stronger private business investment. Business investment is

now estimated to have increased by 1.1% in 2024–25, compared with the initial estimate of a 0.2%
decline. The revisions do not materially alter the broader productivity story, but they do suggest that the
economy was growing a little more strongly for the same rate of inflation in 2025.
above the RBA’s assumption and closer to Treasury’s. The recovery illustrates why it is important to
focus on longer-term trends in productivity growth rather than on the latest year or two, which can be

volatile.
Capital deepening is returning. Investment is picking up and, outside mining, market-sector capital
per hour worked is now at a record high, rising a strong 1.5% in 2025–26.
The reallocation headwind is easing. The shift of labour towards non-market industries, which
generally have lower measured productivity levels, has reduced the level of aggregate productivity by
around 1ppt over the past 15 years. With the expansion of the non-market sector plateauing, this

drag looks set to fade.
Productivity growth is about more than capital. Multifactor productivity, which captures how efficiently
businesses combine labour and capital, was the dominant driver of market-sector productivity growth
in 2025–26. Meanwhile, stronger investment in IT, utilities and professional services should provide a
further lift as new capital is put to work.

### Page 3

The investment upswing is broadening
The Annuals also provide a richer picture of the supply side, including investment by industry and the
evolution of the capital stock, details that are not available in the quarterly accounts. These data point
to a notable lift in investment in the market (ex-mining) part of the economy, where investment has
gone from around 10% of nominal GDP in to just over 11.5%. Outside of this, investment has been
softer, particularly in the mining sector.

### Page 4

Within the market sector, investment by the IT industry, increasingly focused on data centres,
has risen to its highest share of nominal GDP since the technology boom of the 1990s. Utilities
investment, including renewables and associated infrastructure, is stronger still, at its highest share
since 1983, while investment by the professional services industry has reached a record high.

### Page 5

Across the non-market sector (including health, education and public administration), investment has
also held at a higher share of the economy, driven by the health and public administration (including
defence) industries.

### Page 6

Investment is translating into capital deepening
The investment upswing is also beginning to translate into capital deepening as industries look to
rebuild capital stocks after a period of soft growth. Outside mining, market-sector capital per hour
worked is at a record high and well above its pre-pandemic level. That is an encouraging
development, particularly given that the sector accounts for 70% of total employment.
There are important divergences beneath the aggregate. Mining continues to experience capital

shallowing, as investment has been insufficient to cover wear and tear and capital requirements
associated with additional workers. By contrast, the capital stock in the non-market sector appears to
have caught up with the surge in employment we have seen since the pandemic, with capital per
worker increasing for the first time since the pandemic.

### Page 7

A major productivity headwind is starting to fade
Capital deepening will help support productivity growth across the economy. The Annuals show that
productivity in the market sector (excluding mining) continues to grow by around 1% in year-average
terms, while mining remains a drag on aggregate productivity (down 0.8% in 2025–26 and almost 30%
since 2017).

### Page 8

At the same time, a major recent headwind is easing as the rapid expansion of the non-market sector
approaches slows. This matters for aggregate productivity. Productivity growth reflects both gains
within industries, through capital deepening, technology and better processes, and the ‘reallocation’ of
resources between industries with different productivity levels.
Measured productivity in the non-market sector is considerably lower than across much of the market

economy, partly reflecting measurement issues (output in these sectors is generally not well measured,
particularly when there are quality changes). As employment shifted towards these industries, this
compositional effect weighed on aggregate productivity. Our estimates suggest it has reduced the
level of aggregate productivity by a significant 1ppt over the past 15 years and almost 0.3ppts
over the three years to 2025–26.

### Page 9

Productivity gains were about more than capital
It wasn’t only capital deepening that drove productivity growth. Multifactor productivity (MFP), which
captures how efficiently businesses combine labour and capital through changes in processes,
technology, know-how, was the dominant driver of market-sector productivity growth this year. This
gives us more conviction that the supply side is normalising, and productivity growth will improve from
its recent lows.

### Page 10

Where productivity is improving, and where it isn’t
At the industry level, some of those recording stronger investment are also beginning to deliver
stronger productivity outcomes, although the picture remains uneven.
IT stands out at the positive end of the spectrum, alongside improving outcomes in manufacturing,
administrative and support services, and transport.. These are also industries where the diffusion of
AI and other digital technologies could deliver further productivity gains. Utilities and

professional services should also benefit as stronger investment translates into greater capital intensity
and efficiency.
At the other end, mining remains a major drag, consistent with the capital shallowing evident in the
investment and capital-stock data. Construction is another notable laggard, with productivity continuing
to deteriorate despite strong demand from housing, population growth, renewable-energy infrastructure
and data-centre projects. Construction productivity fell around 2% in 2025–26 and almost 21% over the

13 years from 2012–13 to 2025–26.

### Page 11

A more constructive supply-side story
The Annuals provide a more constructive supply-side picture than the aggregate productivity figures
alone suggest. Market-sector productivity outside mining continues to advance, the reallocation drag
from the expanding non-market sector looks likely to fade, and capital deepening is strengthening
across important parts of the economy. The strong MFP outcome in the market sector gives us more
conviction that the supply side is normalising, and productivity growth will improve going ahead.

The critical question is whether the strong investment underway in technology, professional services,
electricity and related infrastructure translates into stronger productivity growth as that new capital is
put to work. If it does, the improvement in capital intensity evident in the Annuals could mark the
beginning of a more durable lift in Australia’s productivity performance.

### Page 12

Related articles

Remain constructive on US healthcare. US healthcare 
earnings are projected to grow almost 23% in 2027, the second-
fastest among MSCI US sectors. We see scope for further 
upside in the sector as innovation underpins long-term earnings 
growth. With the market rotating away from concentrated AI 
exposure, healthcare's stable, non-cyclical demand profile 
offers a compelling exposure as a diversifier (see page 5). 
Outside the US, Switzerland and India offer diversified 
returns. The Swiss equity market consists of 41% healthcare, 
19% financial services and 14% consumer staples, providing a 
defensive alternative to investors seeking to diversify away 
from tech-heavy exposure. The Indian equity market has been 
classified as an “anti-AI” trade. Historically narrow valuation 
premiums vs. EMs, low foreign investor positioning and c. 15% 
expected 2027 earnings growth make it an attractive diversifier. 
Strong jobs and inflation reports cement December Fed 
rate hike. The US economy added 165,000 jobs in October, 
more than twice the 70,000 consensus estimate. The 
unemployment rate fell to 4.1%. Meanwhile, headline and core 
inflation in October, at 0.4% and 0.3% m/m, respectively, 
exceeded expectations as elevated fuel prices, ‘techflation’ and 
earlier tariffs fed into directly exposed sectors, including food, 
utilities and transportation services. A healthy job market keeps 
the Fed focused on lowering inflation, cementing the prospect 
of a December rate hike, potentially followed by another in Q1 
2027. A strong economy and elevated interest rates support our 
overweight allocation to US financial sector equities. 
Locking in gains from our bullish US inflation-protected 
bonds idea. While near-term price pressures remain elevated, 
we expect inflation to ease with energy costs over the coming 
year. Republicans’ heavy losses in the US mid-term elections 
increase pressure on the Trump administration to end the Iran 
conflict. One-year US inflation expectations have slumped 
alongside oil prices. A potential slowdown in the pace of AI 
buildout is also likely to ease techflation. Meanwhile, Fed rate 
hikes should further tighten financial conditions, tempering 
price pressures. This disinflationary outlook makes the risk-
reward for US inflation-protected bonds less compelling. We 
close the idea, locking in a 4.4% gain (page 6). 
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank

WS Global CIO Office | 13 November 2026

What does history tell us about the aftermath of a single-
day sell-off of 4% or more in the S&P500 index?

Our view: The S&P500 fell 4.36% on 5 November 2026, a rare daily 
move of 3.8 standard deviations. Since 2009, there have been only

24 occasions when the index declined by 4% or more in a single

session. The immediate aftermath of such sell-offs has historically

been mixed. One-month returns ranged from around -15% to

+23%, with positive returns in 70% of cases. The weaker

outcomes were concentrated around periods of significant macro

stress, including the tail end of the 2008 Global Financial Crisis

(GFC), the 2020 COVID-19 pandemic and the 2022 monetary policy

tightening cycle. 
The historical picture becomes considerably more constructive

beyond the first month. All 24 episodes generated positive three-
month returns, ranging from roughly +0.5% to +38%, while six-
month returns were positive in 96% of cases, with gains reaching 
as much as +49%. Overall, history suggests sharp sell-offs can be 
followed by near-term volatility, particularly during periods of broader 
macro stress. However, the balance of historical outcomes has 
shifted strongly to the upside over a 3-6-month horizon. 
—  Maggie Au Yeung, Quantitative Analyst 
 o
— Francis Lim, Senior Quantitative Strategist
Where do you see the most favourable risk-reward within

the US technology sector? 
Our view: We favour high-quality Big Tech companies with 
strong balance sheets and prefer diversified exposure through 
our US Information Technology (IT) opportunistic idea. 
Rationale: The recent pullback in US semiconductor stocks has 
improved their risk-reward profile. Earnings for these companies are 
forecast to grow by 65% in 2027, the fastest rate among technology 
subsectors. Despite this outlook, recent regulatory and corporate 
governance concerns are weighing on sentiment. Potential state-
level restrictions on data centre development following the recent 
US mid-term election results could weigh on semiconductor demand 
growth. However, we believe these risks are manageable and 
continued AI adoption should support demand for AI inference. We 
prefer diversified exposure to quality semiconductor 
companies with durable demand and strong volume growth. 
To navigate volatility in the US technology sector, we favour Big Tech 
companies with strong balance sheets and resilient cash generation, 
as they are better positioned to sustain AI investments without 
relying heavily on external financing. Our US IT opportunistic idea 
combines these preferences through exposure to semiconductors, 
software and hardware, allowing investors to participate in a 
potential recovery across a broader range of earnings drivers.
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank

WS Global CIO Office | 13 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d)

Do fundamental drivers support a constructive view on the 
US healthcare sector?

Our view: We remain constructive on US healthcare, supported

by its defensive characteristics and robust earnings growth.

Rationale: US healthcare earnings are projected to grow by 22.5% 
in 2027, the second-fastest among MSCI US sectors, driven by 
improving biopharma earnings. The US healthcare sector's 
valuation gap against the broader market has been recovering from 
a steep historical discount a year ago. The sector's forward price-to-

earnings (P/E) ratio of c. 20x now exceeds the broader US market’s

c. 18.4x, but we see scope for the valuation to be supported amid

investor demand for defensive growth. The sector’s broad legislative

policy overhang has materially faded following the US election cycle,

while targeted regulatory and oversight risks remain on the watchlist.

Healthcare innovation also underpins structural growth. Wider

adoption of obesity and diabetes treatments, alongside advances in

oncology treatments, medical devices and robotic surgery, should

support long-term earnings growth. With the market rotating away

from concentrated AI exposure, healthcare's stable, non-cyclical 
demand profile presents compelling opportunities for investors 
seeking both defensiveness and growth in the near term.
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst
What is your outlook for China’s Big Tech sector? 
Our view: We remain positive on China’s Big Tech sector, 
consistent with our Overweight stance on the country’s IT and 
communication services sector. 
Rationale: Valuations across China’s technology sector remain 
inexpensive at 14.2x, with investor attention centred on whether AI-
driven revenue can offset elevated AI-related spending, which is 
currently compressing margins. Elevated AI capex and negative 
free cash flow remain key investor concerns in the Q3 earnings 
season. Top-line momentum also remains uneven: soft domestic 
consumption continues to weigh on e-commerce, advertising and 
local services, whereas gaming and cloud computing are 
delivering resilient growth. The broader narrative is steadily 
transitioning towards commercialisation, with rapid enterprise 
penetration by AI workplace agents and upcoming AI model 
launches shifting the market focus towards active deployment and 
conversion to paid usage. Evolving regulatory frameworks 
covering AI governance, agent self-regulation, tax scrutiny and 
compliance requirements remain important variables influencing 
medium-term margin trajectories. Ultimately, valuation support, 
gradual monetisation of AI capabilities and resilient core 
segments support our Overweight positioning.
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist
US healthcare sector earnings are projected to 
grow 22.5% in 2027 – the second-fastest among
